Page 13 text:
"ARTICLE I PURPOSE 
The Texas Workforce Commission (TWC) Purchasing Services (PS) section 
will administer the procurement process for this Solicitation, which includes 
Solicitation publication, handling of communications from Respondents, as 
well as managing the receipt of Responses for review and evaluation. 
Information regarding TWC and its programs is available online and can 
currently be accessed at www.twc.texas.gov. 
TWC is seeking to procure administration services for a Central Non-Profit 
Agency (CNA) to organize and administer the State Use Program, as outlined 
in Chapter 122 of the Texas Human Resources Code and Title 40 of the 
Texas Administrative Code (TAC), Chapter 806. 
ARTICLE II SCOPE OF WORK 
2.1 OVERVIEW/BACKGROUND 
a. ... 
b. ... 
c. The Successful Respondent will report directly to TWC. TWC oversees 
the State Use Program, which includes contracting with the Successful 
Respondent (a CNA) to manage the daily operations of the State Use 
Program and provide financial support, including oversight and recruitment 
of Community Rehabilitation Programs (CRPs). 
d. ... 
e. As required by Section 122.009 of the Texas Human Resources Code, to 
the extent the records of the Successful Respondent (a CNA) pertain 
specifically to state purchases of the products and services of persons 
with disabilities, such records are considered to be open records for 
purposes of the Public Information Act, Chapter 552 of the Texas 
Government Code."

So only "The Successful Respondent will report directly to TWC." is a vendor obligation.

Page 14:
"2.2 DESCRIPTION OF SERVICES 
2.2.1 Comprehensive CNA Services 
The Successful Respondent must provide comprehensive CNA Services 
necessary for the effective management and operation of the State Use 
Program, which include, but are not limited to: 
a. CRP Engagement and Development: 
1. Educate potential CRPs on State Use Program participation 
requirements; 
2. Recruit and assist CRPs in developing and submitting applications 
for suitable products and services aligned to customer needs; 
3. Support CRPs with Integrated Competitive Employment efforts and 
compliance with 40 TAC Chapter 806;
4. Assist the CRPs in soliciting and negotiating contracts; and 
5. Provide training and resources to support outplacement/supported 
employment and increase integrated employment opportunities for 
individuals with disabilities. 
b. Program Management and Administration: 
1. Execute a contract agreement with the Statewide Procurement 
Division (SPD) to establish and ensure adherence to appropriate 
statewide terms and conditions for all procurements managed 
under the State Use Program. 
2. Manage and coordinate the day-to-day operation of the program 
including the general administration of contracts with CRPs 
operating pursuant to Chapter 122, Texas Human Resources Code 
and 40 TAC Chapter 806; 
3. Allocate service and product orders among the various CRPs; 
4. Monitor CRPs to ensure compliance with requirements for 
participation in the State Use Program; 
5. Provide accounting services related to purchase orders, invoices, 
and payments to CRPs; 
6. Prepare reports and fulfill other duties as designated by TWC; 
7. Provide general administration of the State Use Program, meet 
performance criteria and submit timely reports required by 40 TAC 
Chapter 806; 
8. Maintain and dispose of records in accordance with applicable laws 
and TWC directives and provide any or all records requested within 
three (3) weeks of the request;
9. Resolve contract issues and/or problems as they arise between the 
CRPs, the Successful Respondent, and/or customers, refer 
unresolved issues to TWC, and submit quarterly status reports on 
issues and referrals; 
10. Maintain a system in accordance with generally accepted accounting 
principles (GAAP) to record information related to purchase orders, 
invoices, and payments to each CRP to facilitate the preparation 
and submission of the annual report; 
11. Demonstrate compliance with state and federal tax laws and payroll 
laws by submitting quarterly reports of sales and taxes paid to CPA 
and the Internal Revenue Service (IRS); and 
12. Maintain expertise of governmental procurement and contracting 
processes and laws. 
c. Product and Service Delivery:  
1. Maintain and operate a retail store and warehouse for use by state 
agency customers, adhering to state statutes and the Comptroller’s 
Statewide Procurement Division requirements; and 
2. Conduct research to identify new products and services. 
d. Marketing and Promotion: 
1. Directly market products and services to consumers through 
advertising and public relations activities as well as a retail business 
operational plan; 
2. Conduct public relations activities to promote the program; 
3. Create a database of state agency and political subdivision 
purchases to promote sales of State Use Program products and 
services;
4. Provide quarterly regional information workshops to promote the 
State Use Program throughout the year and across the state; 
5. Provide customer relations support; and 
6. Provide promotion of increased supported employment 
opportunities for persons with disabilities."

All captured.

Page 15:
"e. Customer Relations and Service Delivery: 
1. Provide superior customer relations by: 
i. monitoring customer satisfaction with products and services,  
ii. responding to customer complaints within one (1) Business 
Day or less, and  
iii. reporting quarterly on consumer satisfaction levels for each 
CRP, based on complaints as to products or services provided, 
with a goal of incurring no more than five (5) complaints per 
year that have not been resolved to customer satisfaction; 
2. Create and maintain automated tracking and monitoring of 
product/service sales and submit quarterly reports to TWC 
regarding delivery turnaround times and contract performance for 
each CRP; and 
3. Respond to inquiries about individual sales and/or total sales within 
five (5) Business Days or sooner and submit quarterly reports 
regarding the number of inquiries and average response time. 
f. Financial Management: Provide sufficient financial resources and/or the 
ability to maintain a line of credit to pay CRPs within thirty (30) Business 
Days. Average weekly payment to CRPs in Fiscal Year 2025 was 
approximately $4.4 million per week. 
g. Compliance and Monitoring:
1. Monitor CRP compliance and promptly report violations to TWC, 
assisting CRPs in achieving compliance as needed;  
2. Conduct business ethically and submit detailed quarterly reports on 
any conflicts between the CRPs and the Successful Respondent; and 
3. Provide financial oversight and monitoring of the CRPs and report 
material changes in the CRP status to TWC. 
h. Training and Resources: Provide training programs to CRPs on the 
requirements to participate in the state use program, governmental 
contracting, and procurement procedures and laws. 
i. Reporting and Transparency: Provide an annual report that includes 
the CNA's audited financial statements, an updated strategic plan, and an 
updated projected schedule of expenses detailing the amount and 
percentage of management fees allocated to: 
1. directly support the state use program, 
2. expand direct services to programs that enhance the lives of 
individuals with disabilities, and 
3. administrative overhead, such as salaries."

All captured.

Page 16:
"j. Expanding Employment Opportunities for Individuals with 
Disabilities: 
1. Promote the development of person-centered planning to document 
employment goals, provide employment counseling, and facilitate 
placement services provided by CRPs, aiming for employment 
paying minimum wage or higher; 
2. Research new products, services, and markets and improve existing 
products and services to increase employment opportunities 
(including those paying minimum wage or higher) and report to 
TWC quarterly on the status of these activities; and
3. Work with CRPs to develop employment opportunities. 
k. Management of Non-Uniformly Priced Services Contracts: The CNA 
shall have the following obligations in connection with Non-Uniformly 
Pirce Services Contracts: 
1. Negotiation Facilitation. The Successful Respondent shall act as the 
primary facilitator between the applicable state agency and the CRP 
to define the site-specific scope of work and pricing. 
2. Pricing Validation. The Successful Respondent must review the 
CRP’s proposed pricing prior to submission to ensure that it 
represents a fair market price, complies with TWC guidelines, and 
covers required wages and administrative fees. 
3. TWC Submission and Approval. The Successful Respondent is solely 
responsible for compiling the completed package (including 
statement of work, pricing schedules, and compliance certifications) 
and submitting it to TWC for statutory review and approval. 
4. Contract Execution and Subcontracting. Upon receiving written 
approval from TWC, the Successful Respondent shall promptly enter 
into a direct contract with the applicable state agency. The 
Successful Respondent shall subsequently execute a subcontract 
with the approved CRP to perform the services, maintaining sole 
prime contractor liability for the CRP’s performance, timely 
payments for services to CRPs, and timely and accurately invoicing 
the applicable state agency for reimbursement of the CNA’s 
payments for the CRP’s services. 
5. Timeliness Standard. The Successful Respondent must submit fully 
validated pricing proposals to TWC within ten (10) Business Days of 
reaching an agreement in principle between the applicable state 
agency and the CRP.
6. Assignment. Contracts for Non-Uniformly Priced Services must 
include a provision permitting assignment to a successor CNA with 
notice to TWC."
